SHERIFF'S OFFICE:
a. Presentation of a Medical Aid Award to Navajo County Sheriff's Office
dispatcher Kendal Webster, and Sheriff's Life-Saving Award(s) to Navajo
County Sheriff's Office Deputy Austin Huddleston and Show Low Police
Department dispatcher Amber Price :
This item was moved to the end of the agenda at the request of Sheriff
Clouse. At 10:11 a.m. Sheriff David Clouse honored three employees;
Deputy Austin Huddleston, dispatcher Kendall Webster, and dispatcher
Amber Price, for their heroic efforts in saving the life of Gary Mitchell, a
community volunteer, who suffered sudden cardiac arrest at home. Sheriff
Clouse stated that Kendall quickly and calmly handled the 911 call,
coordinating with other agencies. Amber provided CPR guidance over the
2 of 7 
DRAFT

<!-- page 8 -->
coordinating with other agencies. Amber provided CPR guidance over the
phone to Mitchell’s wife, and Deputy Huddleston arrived within minutes to
continue life-saving CPR until paramedics arrived. Sheriff Clouse credited
their teamwork and professionalism with saving Mitchell's life and praised
their actions. Each employee was presented with an award. Gary Mitchell
emphasized that he experienced a sudden cardiac arrest, which typically
has only 10% survival rate outside of a hospital. He expressed deep
gratitude to the responders, stating they are the reason he is alive today.
Sheriff Clouse thanked the Board of Supervisors for allowing him to make
the presentation today. Supervisor Seymore praised Mrs. Mitchell for
her excellent efforts performing CPR without prior training, guided by
dispatcher Amber Price for nearly six minutes. He further thanked
everyone involved for their quick, skilled actions that saved Mr. Mitchell’s
life, noting how his survival is a blessing and a testament to the value of
service and teamwork. Supervisor Benally commended the team for
their outstanding lifesaving efforts, emphasizing how difficult CPR is from
personal experience as a former nurse. She expressed gratitude to the
deputies, dispatchers, and all involved for their swift action and community
service, and thanked the sheriff for sharing the story, noting it was a
well-deserved honor. Vice-Chair Whitesinger expressed deep
appreciation for the opportunity to recognize the individuals involved,
praising them as exemplary public servants with compassionate hearts.
She thanked everyone, including the sheriff and chief, for their dedication
to community safety and for allowing this recognition to take place.
 
SUPERINTENDENT OF SCHOOLS: 
 
a. Consideration of approval to enter into an agreement with Legacy
Learning LLC and NCAD (Navajo County Accomodation District) to
provide Dropout Recovery services for students in Navajo County :
Superintendent of Schools Rachael Lewis stated that she is excited to
introduce a new Dropout Recovery Program to the Accommodation
District. She reported a 14% dropout rate in Navajo County and stated
that the program aims to re-engage students who have left high school,
addressing not just personal and societal impacts, but also significant
economic consequences. She estimated at over $400,000 per dropout.
Superintendent Lewis stated that after reviewing three potential providers,
the district chose Legacy Learning Community, a local organization based
in Show Low, due to their strong commitment to personal student support
and understanding of local needs. She stated that the program will use
the district’s accreditation while Legacy provides curriculum and support
services. Superintendent Lewis requested approval to move forward with
this partnership. Supervisor Seymore thanked Superintendent Lewis
for taking a proactive role in trying to help students as he knows that it
does make a difference.  
Supervisor Seymore made a motion to approve entering into an
agreement with Legacy Learning LLC and NCAD (Navajo County
Accommodation District) to provide Dropout Recovery services for
students in Navajo County, motion seconded by Supervisor Peshlakai. 
Motion passed 4-0, with Chairman Whiting being absent.

PUBLIC WORKS:
 
a. Consideration of Award of Contract #B25-03-026 Contract for Hauling
Waste Tires to CRM of America, LLC:
William Flake discussed the item stating that Navajo County Public
Works, in partnership with ADEQ, manages a waste tire recycling
program. He stated that the program involves hauling and disposing of
waste tires from county collection sites to a recycling facility. Mr. Flake
stated that a one-year contract, with the option for four one-year
renewals, was put out for a bid. He further stated that only one bid was
received, and the recommendation is to award the contract to CRM of
America, LLC. Vice-Chair Whitesinger asked what is the average annual
or monthly number of recycled tires? Mr. Flake stated that the average
annual amount, year ending in June of 2024, hauled off 1,414.36 tons of
tires. Vice-Chair Whitesinger emphasized the importance and
effectiveness of the county’s tire recycling program. She spoke of the
recent segment of the County Connection with Show Low TV that she
participated in that showcased the process. She stated that she was
impressed by how accessible and environmentally responsible the
program is. She further stated that the program allows both community
members and local businesses, including tire shops, to easily dispose of
used tires. Vice-Chair Whitesinger expressed her pride in being able to
offer this valuable service to the community.
 
Supervisor Benally made a motion to approve Contract #B25-03-026
Contract for Hauling Waste Tires to CRM of America, LLC, motion
seconded by Supervisor Seymore.  Motion passed 4-0, with Chairman
Whiting being absent.

PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT SERVICES: 
 
a. Update on Renewable Ordinance Discussion:
Mischa Larisch introduces the presentation stating that Mr. Cody Cooper
will provide an overview of the timeline and changes related to the
upcoming renewable ordinance. He further stated that after Cody’s
presentation, he would explain the proposed development agreement
being recommended as part of the process. Cody Cooper provided a
comprehensive overview of Navajo County’s proposed Renewable
Energy Ordinance, which encompasses both solar and wind energy
regulations. Mr. Cooper stated that this was developed through public
outreach and board work sessions, the ordinance establishes general
development standards for both energy types, along with specific sections
tailored to each. He further stated that it introduces a development
agreement option that may be used alongside or instead of a special use
permit and includes preferred criteria such as placing projects on less
desirable land. Mr. Cooper reported on the stronger fire safety language
that has been codified, ensuring local fire agencies are involved in the
review and construction processes. He reported on the enhanced road
use agreements, stating that they will now cover both pre- and
post-construction phases. Mr. Cooper stated that the ordinance will also
require project developers to consult with nearby municipalities or tribal
entities within a five-mile radius and further mandates the use of the best
commercially available technology to minimize environmental impacts.
Mr. Cooper reported on the environmental, cultural, wetland, and visual
impact studies that will be required for all renewable projects. He stated
that additional provisions include setback waivers for non-participating
properties, adjusted setback and screening standards for solar
developments, and a fee structure specific to renewable energy projects
due to their complexity. Mr. Cooper closed by stating that originally
Planning and Development expected that this item would be brought
forward to the Board of Supervisors at the July meeting, however the
ordinance is now set to be presented on June 24, ahead of schedule, with
the potential for adoption by the end of next month.  Supervisor
Seymore thanked Mr. Cooper for working hard on the item and
expressed his appreciation for all the efforts put into the ordinance.
Mischa Larisch provided an overview of development agreements, which
are contracts between a county and a developer or landowner that define
specific terms and conditions for a development project. Mr. Larisch
stated that these agreements are common across several Arizona
  
                                                                    5 of 7 
DRAFT

<!-- page 11 -->
counties, and often address aspects like construction phases,
infrastructure requirements, and alignment with the county’s
comprehensive plan. Mr. Larisch reported that the proposal includes
amending the zoning ordinance to allow the development agreements to
either in place of or alongside special use permits. He stated that
development agreements would be used for larger projects and special
use permits would be used for smaller ones. Mr. Larisch explained that a
development agreement becomes legally binding once approved by the
Board of Supervisors and recorded with the County Recorder’s Office. He
further reviewed key components of a development agreement, which
include identification of involved parties, property descriptions, terms and
conditions (such as duration, permitted uses, density, infrastructure
needs, and development phases), and any other project-specific
requirements. Once recorded, development agreements offer benefits like
certainty, stability, predictability, and improved collaboration between
developers and the county. Mr. Larisch stated that the proposed process
would involve legal counsel in developing a standardized template, and
developers would work closely with county staff, administration, and legal
teams to finalize agreements. Mr. Larisch indicated that Supervisors from
the project’s district may participate in negotiations, and public
involvement will be preserved through required community input meetings
and opportunities for public comment at Board meetings before adoption.
Supervisor Seymore asked whether development agreements, like
special use permits, which are typically reviewed every two to three
years, can include similar review conditions, and whether the process for
approving a development agreement still requires going through the
Planning and Zoning Commission or if it bypasses that step and goes
directly to the Board of Supervisors through internal collaboration? Mischa
Larisch explained that while the development agreement process still
includes public input, it does not go through the Planning and Zoning
Commission. Mr. Larisch indicated that instead, it is streamlined directly
through the Board of Supervisors, making the process more efficient.
Vice-Chair Whitesinger expressed her gratitude and echoed the
sentiments of Supervisor Seymore, appreciating the thorough and
thoughtful development of the process. She emphasized the importance
of collaboration, especially in addressing unexpected needs and ensuring
all aspects, such as emergency access and partnerships with tribal and
non-tribal communities, are considered. Vice-Chair Whitesinger
commended the effort to create a strong, inclusive policy that supports
investment and partnership within Navajo County.

memorial services honoring fallen officers in Navajo County, emphasizing the

outstanding students were recognized for both academic excellence and

teachers, parents, and students in achieving success, reflecting positively on the
education system. He concluded with a reflective message encouraging people to

let their good deeds shine and be seen, hoping that the good work of Navajo

including presenting his first quarterly report to six chapters and helping organize a
successful produce distribution event in partnership with nonprofit organizations,
serving 703 people. Another distribution occurred the same day in a northern area
of the district. Supervisor Peshlakai attended the Navajo County Sheriff's
Detention Academy graduation and Holbrook High School’s graduation and

guest speaker at the White Cone Veterans event and supported Elder Day
celebrations in two locations. He concluded by expressing gratitude to staff for
their behind-the-scenes support and looks forward to continuing outreach in the

traveling between them, and congratulated all graduates, wishing them safety and
success in their future endeavors. She also participated in an Elders celebration at

cherished two special times of the year: the first day of school and graduation. She
further stated that she loved seeing the excitement and hope in young children
starting school and prays that their dreams come true. Vice-Chair Whitesinger
stated that she valued witnessing graduations, recognizing the hard work and
challenges students have overcome to reach that milestone. She stated that the
experience renews her commitment to supporting young people by creating
opportunities where they feel valued and empowered for success. Vice-Chair
Whitesinger ended with a sentimental note of appreciation for being able to share
her thoughts.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. c.        
Meeting Date: 06/10/2025  
Title: BJA COPE Legal Defense Contract with Law Offices of Jo Ellen Vector PLLC
Submitted For: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney  Submitted By: Lynda Young, Office Manager
Department: County Attorney
Motion before the Board:
COPE Contract for Legal Defense with the Law Offices of Jo Ellen Vork PLLC
Background:
Individuals at risk of overdose are particularly concentrated in the justice system, where an estimated 33
percent of arrestees and 65 percent of prisoners have a diagnosable substance use disorder. Therefore, it
is incumbent upon the justice system to take action to reduce overdose risk and protect lives. 
In one three-week period, 30 inmates identified as opioid users and 14 of those inmates indicated that
they have overdosed at least once.  The County Overdose Prevention and Engagement (COPE) program
will be offered to inmates who meet certain criteria and volunteer to enroll in the program.  This is part of
a rapid response team of justice system and behavioral health practitioners to identify individuals at risk
of drug overdose immediately after arrest and link them with evidence-based treatment, recovery support
services, and ongoing monitoring. The goal of the program is to prevent overdose deaths and increase
long-term treatment engagement among at-risk individuals.
 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY25/26
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 145,000
Fiscal Impact:
Expenses to be 100% reimbursed through the BJA COPE Grant #15PBJA-23-GG-05258-VTCX.

2. CONTRACT DOCUMENTS: The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and the 
attached ☒ Request for Proposal #B25-03-019, ☒ Consultant’s Proposal, and 
addenda thereto, if any.  If there is any conflict in the documents, this Contract shall 
control over all others; the Request for Proposal shall control over the Consultant’s 
proposal. 
 
3. TIME FOR COMPLETION : Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner 
consistent with the degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the 
same profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s 
services shall be performed as expeditiou sly as is consistent with professional skill 
and care and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accordance with an agreed 
upon schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established 
by a schedule approve d by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be 
exceeded by County or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as 
the Project proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number 
of days that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures 
or other causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing 
by the parties’ representatives. 
 
4. CANCELLATION: This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38 -
511, (concerning conflicts of interest). 
 
5. PAYMENTS:  County shall pay to Consultant one hundred dollars ($81.25) per hour 
billed in two -tenths (.2) of an hour increment , as payment in full for all services 
rendered by Consultant pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made within 
30 days of County’s receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall detail 
the work performed during the billing period.  Payments shall be made based on actual 
hours worked and approved by the county not to exceed $5,000.00 per month. 
 
6. CERTIFICATIONS: 
 
A. PROHIBITION OF BOYCOTT OF ISRAEL:  Consultant certifies that neither it 
nor its subcontractors are currently or will be engaged in, for the duration of this 
Contract engage in, a boycott of Israel as defined by A.R.S. § 35 -393 et seq.

<!-- page 15 -->
C-2 
Violation of this certification may result in an action by the County up to and 
including termination of the Contract. 
 
B. WRITTEN CERTIFICATION PURSUANT TO A.R.S. §35 -394:  Pursuant to 
A.R.S. § 35-394, Consultant certifies that it is not currently using, and agrees for 
the duration of this Agreement to not use (1) the forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs 
in the People’s Republic of China; (2) any goods or services produced by the 
forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the Pe ople’s Republic of China; and (3) any 
contractors, subcontractors, or suppliers that use the forced labor of ethnic 
Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of China.  The Consultant further 
acknowledges and agrees that (1) if the Consultant becomes aware during the 
term of this Agreement that it is not in compliance with this certification that the 
Consultant will notify the County within five (5) business days after becoming 
aware of the noncompliance; and (2) if the Consultant does not provide the 
County with a written certification that the Consultant has remedied the 
noncompliance within one hundred eighty (180) after giving notice thereof, the 
same shall constitute a material breach of this Agreement, which shall then 
terminate automatically, except that if the Agreement termination date occurs 
before the end of the remedy period, this Agreement terminates on the 
Agreement’s termination date.  The County retains the legal right to inspect the 
records of the Consultant and all Subcontractors to ensure compliance with this 
certification for the duration of this Agreement.

Title: Juvenile Diversion Program No. B23-01-003 Amendment No. 3
Submitted For: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney

Department: County Attorney
Motion before the Board:
Juvenile Diversion Program, B23-01-003 Contract Amendment No. 3 extending the contract with Advent
Financial Systems, LLC. for approximately eight and one-half months (July 1, 2025, through March 13,
2026) and modifying the contract pricing effective July 1, 2025
Background:
On March 14, 2023, the Board of Supervisors awarded Contract B23-01-003 to Advent Financial
Systems, LLC. for the Juvenile Diversion Program. This contract has worked well for Navajo County.
The Juvenile Diversion Program contract may be renewed for up to four additional one-year periods or
portions thereof. The Board of Supervisors approved Amendment No. 2 on March 11, 2025, extending
the contract from March 14, 2025, to June 30, 2025. This would be the third renewal and would extend
the contract through March 13, 2026. This amendment also replaces the Price Page effective July 1, 2025. 
Both parties are in agreement to extend the contract through March 13, 2026, utilizing the updated pricing.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. i.        
Meeting Date: 06/10/2025  
Title: Annual Newspaper Advertising and Printing #B25-04-016
Submitted By: Kathleen Outland, Procurement Trainee
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Award of Contract #B25-04-016 Annual Newspaper Advertising and Printing Contract to Painted Desert
Media Group, LLC dba The Tribune
Background:
This is an annual contract for newspaper advertising and printing which is required per Statute A.R.S.
11-255.  The sealed bids were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Government
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m M.S.T, May 22, 2025.  The bids were publicly opened and read
aloud at 3:15 p.m. M.S.T. Two bids were received and Painted Desert Media Group, LLC., dba The
Tribune was the lowest responsive, responsible bidder, with a bid of $4.50 per column inch.
Staff recommends the award of this contract to Painted Desert Media Group, LLC., dba The Tribune.

 CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES 
 
 
AGREEMENT made as of June 10, 2025, effective between July 1, 2025 through 
June 30, 2026 between Navajo County ("County") and Painted Desert Media Group, LLC 
(“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, County requires certain professional services for Annual Advertising 
and Printing Contract (“Project”); with the contents of ARS§ 11 -217, 11-254.01, 39- 201 
through 39-205, and 39-221. 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform all work for Annual Contract for Advertising 
and Printing, Contract #B25-04-016, and those additional items set forth in the 
Consultant’s offer and letter dated May 6, 2025, in a competent and professional 
manner to the satisfaction of the County. The Annual Contract for Advertising 
and Printing, B25-04-016 and the Consultant’s Proposal are attached hereto and 
by this reference incorporated herein. If any incorporated term is inconsistent 
with the Agreement, this Agreement shall control. 
 
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perform this agreement. Consultant shall 
self-perform this agreement and be devoted to the successful accomplishment of 
the work to be performed hereunder. Once work commences on this agreement, 
Consultant shall adhere to agreed upon timeline and commitment to successful 
completion of the contracted services. 
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agent of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be withheld from any payment by County, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 
9, 10, 12, 15, 18, 21, and 22.  Consultant is responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of car e and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accordance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
County or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
6. Payment.  County shall pay to Consultant a not-to-exceed sum of four dollars and 
fifty cents ($4.50) per column inch as payment in full for all services rendered by 
Consultant pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shall be made within 30 days of 
County’s receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall detail the work 
performed during the billing period.  Invoice amounts in percent of Project cost shall 
not exceed the percentage of completion of the Project as approved by County.   
 
7. Term of Contract. July 1, 2025 thru June 30, 2026 
 
8. Defects in Work.  County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifications.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal negotiation as set forth in Section 1 3.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any rejected work until such issues have been 
resolved. 
 
9. Insurance Requirements.  There are no insurance requirements for this contract. 
 
10. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable by law, Consultant shall defend, 
indemnify and hold harmless County and its representatives, officers, officials and 
employees (the “Indemnitees”) from and against any and all claims, damages, losses 
and expenses (including but not limited to attorney fees, court costs, and the costs of 
appellate proceedings) relating to, arising out of or resulting from Consultant’s 
negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this Agreement. 
Consultant’s duty to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise 
in connection with any claim, damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily

<!-- page 53 -->
B25-04-016 
Page -3- 
 
injury, sickness, disease, death or injury to, impairment or destruction of property 
including loss of use resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent 
act, error, mistake or omission in the performance of this Agreement (including those 
by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may 
be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of 
insurance coverage requirements set forth above shall not limit the scope of the 
indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
11. Records.  Consultant shall retain and shall require each subcontractor (if applicable) 
to retain, all books, accounts, reports, files and other records relating to this 
Agreement for a period of five years after completion of the work. All such 
documents shall be subject to inspection and audit by County upon reasonable notice 
during normal business hours. A legible copy of any or all such documents shall be 
produced by Consultant at the request and expense of County. 
 
12. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement 
without County’s prior written approval, nor shall Consultant delegate any duty 
hereunder without County’s prior written approval.   
 
13. Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
claim or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be provided in writing, with supporting documentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 24.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes 
within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available 
legal remedies. 
 
14. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
A. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to 
suspend, delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment 
shall be made to the completion date and for any increase in the cost of 
performance resulting from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No 
adjustment shall be made to the extent that performance is or would have been 
suspended, delayed or interrupted by another cause for which Consultant is 
responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.  County may terminate this Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to 
perform this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; 
disregards laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or orders of a public authority 
having jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of this Agreement. 
 When any of the foregoing causes exist, County shall give Consultant written 
notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains 
uncured, County may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may 
have) terminate this Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  
If the unpaid balance of the Agreement exceeds the cost of finishing the work

(including any expenses incurred by County as a result of Consultant’s 
default), Consultant shall be entitled to payment for its performance up to the 
amount of such excess.  If the cost of finishing the work exceeds the unpaid 
balance, Consultant shall pay the difference to County. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.  County, by written notice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of 
County it is in County’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be paid for all 
material, equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination 
expenses and a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that 
such payments, exclusive of termination expenses, shall not exceed the total 
amount payable pursuant to Section 6 less any payments previously made to 
Consultant.  Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on 
material, equipment and services which were not provided. 
 
D. Consultant’s Responsibilities.  Upon receipt of a termination notice,  
Consultant shall (a) promptly discontinue all services (unless the notice directs 
otherwise), and (b) deliver or otherwise make available to County copies of 
all data, reports, summaries and other information and materials developed or 
accumulated by Consultant in performing this Agreement. 
 
15. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to County if performance is made impossible for a period of 30 consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by a court or other 
public authority having jurisdiction; an act of government, such as a declaration of 
national emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also

interruptions by County equal in the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of 
days scheduled for completion. 
 
16. Governing Law.  This Agreement shall be governed by the law of the State of

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. j.        
Meeting Date: 06/10/2025  
Title: Holbrook USD #3 Ward 3 Board Member Special Election
Submitted By: Rachael Lewis, Superintendent of Schools
Department: Superintendent of Schools
Motion before the Board:
Conduct a special election for 1 board member for Holbrook USD #3, Ward 3 on November 4, 2025
Background:
The Holbrook USD #3 Ward boundaries have recently been redrawn. As Ward 3 has a vacant seat, in
cooperation with Holbrook USD, the County Superintendent's Office has found it prudent to hold a
special election in order to fill the vacancy rather than appoint a governing board member. This will allow
voters that reside in Ward 3 to elect a board member of their choosing.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2026
Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested: 0.00
Fiscal Impact:
The Recorders/Elections Office has provided a quote of $3,140.00 to conduct an election by mail for
Holbrook USD #3 Ward 3 only. Holbrook USD #3 has verbally confirmed that they will pay for the cost
of the election.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. k.        
Meeting Date: 06/10/2025  
Title: Agreement for Northeastern Arizona Law Enforcement Training Academy
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Agreement with Navjo County Community College District dba Northland Pioneer College (The
College), Northeastern Arizona Law Enforcement Training Academy (NALETA) at Northeast Arizona
Training Center (NATC), Navajo County, City of Show Low, Town of Taylor, City of Snowflake, Town
of Pinetop-Lakeside, City of Holbrook, City of Winslow, Town of Eagar, Town of Springerville, Apache
County, and the White Mountain Apache Tribe (Member Agencies) to provide Northeastern Arizona
with a police academy called NALETA that can provide certified AZPOST training to students
Background:
The purpose of this agreement is to provide Northeastern Arizona with a police academy, hereby called
NALETA that can provide certified AZPOST (Arizona Peace Officer Standards and Training) training to
students who meet AZPOST qualifications and are sponsored by a Law Enforcement Agency. Member
and non-member agencies may enroll employees in NALETA. The college shall compensate the member
agency employing the class supervisor fifty-thousand dollars ($50,000) per fiscal year, with member
agencies contributing an annual payment of $10,000 to the NATC. Non-member agencies shall pay
$4,000 per student for attendance of recruits and $2,500 annually for access to the NALETA facility at
NATC. This agreement shall be effective from July 1, 2025, and continue through June 30, 2031.

AGREEMENT FOR
NORTHEASTERN ARIZONA LAW ENFORCEMENT TRAINING ACADEMY
AT NORTHEAST ARIZONA TRAINING CENTER
This Agreement (“Agreement”) is made pursuant to Arizona Revised Statutes (“A.R.S.”)
among Navajo County Community College District dba Northland Pioneer College (“College”),
Northeast Arizona Training Center, a non-profit corporation (“NATC”), and Navajo County,
City of Show Low, Town of Taylor, City of Snowflake, Town of Pinetop-Lakeside, City of
Holbrook, City of Winslow, Town of Eagar, Town of Springerville, Apache County, and the
White Mountain Apache Tribe.  Collectively, the cities, towns, tribes and municipalities signing
on to this Agreement shall be known as the “Member Agencies” and individually as a
“Member.”
Section 1. Purpose.
 The purpose of this Agreement is to provide Northeastern Arizona with a Police
Academy hereby called Northeastern Arizona Law Enforcement Training Academy
(“NALETA”) that can provide certified AZPOST (“Arizona Peace Officer Standards and
Training”) training to students who meet AZPOST qualifications and are sponsored by a Law
Enforcement agency.
Section 2.  Enrollment.
Member Agencies and non-member agencies may enroll employees in NALETA to the
extent that the employees are qualified for enrollment as set forth by AZPOST Rules and
Procedures Manual as set under the State of Arizona Rules of Authority A.R.S. §§ 41-1821
through 41-1828.01 and Arizona Administrative Code, Title 13-4-101 through 13-4-118 and that
NALETA has the capacity to train those employees. College shall not be required to accept any
employee for enrollment unless the city, town, tribe, or county responsible for that employee has
conducted any required background checks on the employee at its own expense as set forth by
AZPOST regulations.
Section 3. Duties and Responsibilities of College. College shall operate and administer
NALETA at the NATC. College’s operational and administrative responsibilities shall include,
but are not limited to:
3.1 Curriculum Development:  Developing and providing the AZPOST curriculum
for use at NALETA.
3.2 Program Administration:  Scheduling courses, registering students, and
administering student transcripts for NALETA courses.
3.3 Instructor Support:  Assisting instructors at NALETA.
3.4. Insurance and Liability:  Obtaining and maintaining adequate insurance coverage
to protect against liabilities arising from damage to persons or property resulting from the
operation of NALETA
3.5 Class Supervisor Funding:  The College shall compensate the Member employing the
Class Supervisor in the amount of fifty thousand dollars ($50,000.00) per fiscal year. Payment shall be
made directly to the employing Member within thirty (30) calendar days of the submission of a proper

<!-- page 62 -->
Page 2 of 6
invoice. In no event shall the College’s total payment obligation under this provision exceed fifty
thousand dollars ($50,000.00) for that fiscal year.
3.6 Class Availability:  College shall have no obligation to conduct any class for which
Members fail to provide qualified instructors who meet all necessary certification and training
requirements as required by AZPOST Rules and Procedures, including Class Supervisor and
Recruit Training Officer(s) (RTO) in accordance with the requirements in Section 4.
Section 4. Duties and Responsibilities of Member Agencies.
4.1 Instructor Provision:  Member Agencies shall provide qualified instructors for all
classes offered under this Agreement, including the Class Supervisor and RTO specified below,
and any expert as may be required under the AZPOST Rules and Procedures. Such instructors
must meet all applicable certification and training requirements as determined by AZPOST Rules
and Procedures, including certification as a Arizona Peace Officer Standards and Training
(AZPOST) General Instructor.
4.2 Instructor Compensation:  Except for the College’s payment obligation set forth in
Section 3.5 above, compensation for instructors shall be the sole responsibility of the Member
Agency employing said instructors. No party to this Agreement, other than the employing agency,
shall have any liability for instructor compensation.
4.3 Staffing Requirements and Cooperation.  The Parties understand and agree that
each class requires the following minimum staff:
 Class Supervisor
 Recruit Training Officer 1 (RTO 1)
 Recruit Training Officer 2 (RTO 2), as needed to maintain the ratio
described in 4.3(b), below.
a. Class Supervisor Provision: Member Agencies shall provide, on a determined
rotation agreed to by and between the Member Agencies, a Class Supervisor (not
necessarily holding the rank of "sergeant") who must be vetted and selected by
representatives of the Member Agencies. This commitment is expected to be
fulfilled for a period of 36 months.
b. RTO Provision: Member Agencies shall provide a sufficient number of Recruit
Training Officers (RTOs) to ensure that each class maintains the ratio of not more
than one (1) RTO per ten (10) students/cadets.
i. The Navajo County Sheriff's Office (NCSO) shall provide RTO 1 for
each class during the term of this Agreement. NCSO shall be exempt from
providing any additional staff (such as Class Supervisor) other than those
scheduled as specific class instructors
ii. If a second RTO is needed for any class, RTO 2 shall be assigned on a one-
year rotational basis.
c. Member Agency Cooperation.  A rotational matrix for Class Supervisors and
RTOs shall be established and adhered to by all Member Agencies entering this
Agreement. In the event that a Member Agency is unable to fulfill its staffing
obligations under the rotational matrix, that Member Agency shall be responsible
for coordinating an appropriate replacement staff member.

<!-- page 63 -->
Page 3 of 6
Section 5. Financial Obligations:
5.1 MEMBER Agencies:  Each Member Agency shall contribute an annual payment of
$10,000.00 to the Northern Arizona Training Center (NATC). This contribution provides all-inclusive
access to the Northern Arizona Law Enforcement Training Academy (NALETA) facility and permits
unlimited attendance of police recruits from the Member Agency at training sessions conducted at the
academy.
The totality of this contribution shall be used by NATC exclusively for purposes that support and
sustain NALETA operations. These purposes shall include, but are not limited to:
 Offsetting costs incurred by the Member Agency that provides the class supervisor for each
academy class;
 Covering routine maintenance and repair of academy facilities to ensure safety, functionality, and
operational readiness;
 Acquiring technology tools and instructional resources to enhance academy training capabilities;
and
 Ensuring that the academy and its associated infrastructure are properly maintained and
operationally supported in accordance with professional training standards.
NATC shall ensure transparency and fiscal accountability in the use of these funds and shall make
available upon request a financial summary of expenditures related to this contribution to NPC or any
Member Agency within thirty (30) calendar days of such request.
5.2 NON-MEMBER Agencies:  Shall pay $4000.00 per student for attendance of police
recruits at NALETA and $2500.00 annually for access to the NALETA facility at NATC.  All
payment shall be made to NATC.
5.3 College fees and charges.   The College shall charge and waive each student enrolled in
the AJS102 “Intensive Police Academy” class held at NALETA a nonrefundable program and
media fee based on the current class fee schedule. Any fees that are charged and not waived shall
belong to College. Any reimbursement received from AZPOST for the successful completion of
NALETA by a candidate shall belong to the District.
5.4.  Other fees or costs.  Except as otherwise specified in this Agreement, each party shall
be responsible for whatever costs that party incurs in connection with this Agreement.
Section 6. Term and termination. This Agreement shall be effective from July 1, 2025 and continue
through June 30, 2031. Any party may terminate its participation into Agreement as of the end of
any fiscal year by providing at least thirty (30) days prior written notice of its intention to do so to the
other parties. Such early termination shall be effective only at the end of the fiscal year in which such notice
is given and only as to the party terminating. Agreement of all parties is required for termination of the
Agreement.
Section 7.  Miscellaneous.
7.1 Immigration compliance. As required by A.R.S. § 41-4401, each party certifies
that it and all of its subcontractors, if any, are in compliance with federal immigration laws and
regulations that relate to their employees and with A.R.S. § 23-214(A). A breach of this warranty
shall be deemed a material breach of this Agreement and shall be subject to penalties up to and
including termination of this Agreement. Each party shall have the right to inspect the papers of
the other party and of any subcontractors to ensure that this warranty is being complied with.

<!-- page 64 -->
Page 4 of 6
7.2 Conflicts of interest. As required by A.R.S. § 38-511, each party gives notice as
follows that it may, within three years after its execution, cancel this Agreement, without penalty
or further obligation, if any person significantly involved in initiating, negotiating, securing,
drafting or creating the Agreement on behalf of the party is, at any time while the Agreement or
any extension of the Agreement is in effect, an employee or agent of any other party to the
Agreement in any capacity or a consultant to any other party of the Agreement with respect to
the subject matter of the Agreement.
7.3 Entire Agreement; Amendments. This Agreement represents the entire
Agreement of the Parties with respect to its subject matter. This Agreement shall not be changed,
modified, or rescinded, except through a writing signed by all parties.
7.4 Governing Law, Forum. This Agreement will be governed by the laws of the
State of Arizona, both as to interpretation and performance. Any judicial proceeding for the
enforcement of this Agreement or any provision thereof shall be instituted only the courts of
Navajo County, State of Arizona, provided that nothing herein shall be deemed a waiver of
either explicit nor implicit of the parties’ sovereign immunity from suit.
7.5 Insurance. The participants will ensure that all parties will protect the other
participants by providing insurance coverage in an amount no less than $1,000,000 and naming
each participant as an individual insured with the proper endorsements.
7.6 Indemnification.  To the extent permitted by law, each party agrees (as indemnitor)
to indemnify, defend and hold harmless the other party (as indemnitee) from and against any and
all claims, losses, liability, costs, or expenses (including reasonable attorney’s fees) (collectively
(“Claims”) arising out of bodily injury of any person (including death) or property damage, but
only to the extent that such claims are caused by the act, omission or negligence, misconduct, or
other fault of the indemnitor, its officers, officials, agents, employees or volunteers. If a Claim or
Claims by third parties become subject to this indemnity provision, the parties to this Agreement
that are the subject of such Claim or Claims shall expeditiously meet to discuss a common and
mutual defense, including possible proportional liability and proportional payment of possible
litigation expenses and money damages. The obligations under this Section shall survive
termination of this Agreement.
7.7 No Joint Venture. This Agreement is not intended to constitute, create, give rise to,
or otherwise recognize a joint venture agreement, partnership or other formal business association
or organization of any kind, and the rights and obligations of the Parties shall be only those
expressly set forth in this Agreement.
7.8 Workman’s Compensation. For purposes of workers’ compensation, an
employee of a Party to this Agreement, who works under the jurisdiction or control of, or who
works within the jurisdictional boundaries of another Party pursuant to this specific Agreement,
is deemed to be an employee of both the Party who is his primary employer and the Party under
whose jurisdiction or control or within whose jurisdictional boundaries he is then working, as
provided in A.R.S. § 23-1022(D). The primary employer Party of such employee shall be solely
liable for payment of workers’ compensation benefits for the purposes of this section. Each
Party herein shall comply with the provisions of A.R.S. § 23-1022(E) by posting the public
notice required.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. l.        
Meeting Date: 06/10/2025  
Title: Arizona Dept of Administration 9-1-1 Program Grant Agreement
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
AZDOA Arizona 9-1-1 program grant agreement, grant number ADOA-AZ911-26-34, between the
Northern Arizona User's Association acting as System Administrator, and the Arizona Department of
Administration, for the administration of the emergency telecommunications services revolving fund
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office Dispatch Supervisor is the System Administrator for the Navajo and
Apache County's Arizona Department of Administration (ADOA) State 9-1-1 program. One of the
primary duties of the System Administrator is processing all bills associated with the 9-1-1 network,
maintenance, 9-1-1 equipment, and special projects funded by State 9-1-1.
The 9-1-1 bills are for the communications centers of Navajo County Sheriff's Office, Apache County
Sheriff's Office, Snowflake-Taylor Police Department, Show Low Police Department, Pinetop-Lakeside
Police Department, the White Mountain Apache Tribe, and Winslow Police Department.
This agreement outlines the responsibilities of the Sheriff's Office for payment of 9-1-1 related
expenditures in the amount of $207,390. This agreement will commence on 7/1/20254 and terminate on
6/30/2026.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. o.        
Meeting Date: 06/10/2025  
Title: Application for Fireworks Display Heber-Overgaard Chamber of Commerce
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Application for supervised fireworks display permit submitted by the Heber-Overgaard Chamber of
Commerce
Background:
The event is scheduled for Saturday, July 5, 2025, from 9:00 p.m. to 10:00 p.m. at Motollon High School,
2450 Mustang Avenue, Heber, Az 85928. 
The Navajo County Sheriff is in support of this event and the Certificate of Sheriff is attached. 
Our Emergency Management Director attended the initial planning meeting that was held on May 7th. It
should be noted that we are currently under Stage 2 fire restrictions throughout the region, including the
Apache Sitgreaves Forests. However, the USFS has not initiated forest closures. Chief Payne was also in
attendance at that meeting and is aware of the fire restrictions. Everyone at the meeting understood that
the final decision could be made as late as the day of the fireworks display about whether they would be
allowed to proceed.  Barring any unforeseen issues, our Emergency Management Director offered support
for the application with the understanding that a final decision could change with little notice depending
on conditions, resource availability and support availability.

Dear Chief Payne and Members of the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, 
 
This letter is to express our support for the Heber-Overgaard Chamber of Commerce's 
application for a supervised fireworks display permit for their July 4th Celebration Firework 
Show, scheduled for Saturday, July 5, 2025.  The event will take place at the Mogollon High 
School Football Field, located at 34350 Mustang Road, Heber, AZ.  The event is scheduled from 
4:00 PM to 11:30 PM, with the fireworks display occurring between approximately 9:00 PM and 
10:00 PM. 
I have reviewed the permit application and communicated with Heber-Overgaard Fire Chief 
Jason Payne regarding the safety plan for July 5, 2025.  The required proof of bond or insurance 
was included in the application.  I have also reviewed the display area map and acknowledge that 
Fire Chief Jason Payne will serve as the Chief Operating Officer for the fireworks, with Craig 
Beaufeaux as the Certified Pyrotechnic Technician. 
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office will manage traffic flow along Highway 260 and the detour 
route to ensure public safety during the event.  The Navajo County Public Works Department has 
provided a traffic control plan, which has been submitted and approved by the county. 
The Navajo County Sheriff’s Office will ensure all detours and traffic control measures related to 
the July 5, 2025, event are adequately staffed, and necessary adjustments will be made to 
maintain the safety of motorists and manage crowd control within the event area. 
Please do not hesitate to contact me if you have any questions or require further information 
regarding this operation. 
 
Sincerely, 
LT. Jerry VanWinkle

Title: IGA -SEP-NAC-060125-01, Agreement provide funding Renewables Coordinator
Submitted For: Chris Pasterz, Economic Development Director 
Submitted By: Chris Pasterz, Economic Development Director
Department: Administrative Services Division: Economic Development
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement, IGA -SEP-NAC-060125-01, to provide funding for the Renewables
Coordinator, aka: Navajo County Coal Community Development Manager
Background:
The purpose of this Agreement is to provide funding for the support of a Renewables Coordinator
aka, Navajo County Coal Community Development Manager, serving under the direction of the Navajo
County Economic Development Director. The Renewables Coordinator will manage relationships with
and support the development process for companies and utilities interested in siting and constructing
Coal Community Transition projects and clean energy technology projects in Navajo and Apache Counties.
This is a 2-year fixed-term position only, non-exempt for the period of July 1,2025 - September 30, 2027.
There is no expectation that this position will become a full-time position after this IGA period is
complete. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY25/26 
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 270,111.00
Fiscal Impact:
This IGA is for full reimbursement for the fixed 2-year position reporting to the Navajo County Economic
Development Director. 
This IGA is for the total amount of $270,111.00 over two financial years, FY25/26 and FY26/27.

Agreement No. IGA-SEP-NAC-060125-01 | Page 1 of 7 
 
 
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT 
No. IGA-SEP-NAC-060125-01 
 
Between the 
 
STATE OF ARIZONA, 
THE OFFICE OF THE GOVERNOR 
 
And the 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY 
 
I. RECITALS 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 41-101.01 authorizes the State of Arizona, Office of the Governor (“GVA”) to accept and 
administer funds and is charged with the responsibility of administering the United States Department of Energy 
(“DOE”) State Energy Program (“SEP”) award num ber DE-EE0010058. The Catalog of Federal Domestic 
Assistance (“CFDA”)/Assistance Listing for all activity pursuant to this agreement is 81.041. 
 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. §11- 952 authorizes public agencies to enter into agreements to contract for services, to 
jointly exercise any powers common to the public agencies, and for joint or cooperative action. 
 
THEREFORE, it is agreed that the GVA and the Navajo County (“Grantee”), hereafter collectively referred to 
as the “Parties,” enter into the terms of this Intergovernmental Agreement (“Agreement”). 
 
II. PURPOSE OF AGREEMENT 
The purpose of this Agreement is to provide funding for the support of a Renewables Coordinator, serving under 
the direction of the Navajo County Economic Development Director. The Renewables Coordinator will manage 
relationships with and support the development process for companies and utilities interested in siting and 
constructing Coal Community Transition projects and clean energy technology projects in Navajo and Apache 
Counties. 
 
The Grantee agrees to comply with all applicable state statutes, regulations, policies, guidelines and 
requirements, including administrative requirements, with the use of this funding. The Office of the Governor 
will provide the financial, programmatic, and administrative guidelines and statutory program purposes for the 
program. The Grantee agrees to comply with all lawful requirements imposed by the GVA in the administration 
of these grant funds. 
 
III. TERM OF AGREEMENT, TERMINATION AND AMENDMENTS 
This Agreement shall be effective June 1, 2025 and shall terminate on September 30, 2027, contingent upon 
funding. 
 
Either party may terminate this Agreement at any earlier time by providing written notice to the other party at 
least thirty (30) days prior to the termination date. The GVA agrees that regardless of its termination date with 
Grantee, Grantee may use the funds distributed under this Agreement to pay for any unpaid services pursuant

<!-- page 109 -->
Agreement No. IGA-SEP-NAC-060125-01 | Page 2 of 7 
to this Agreement obligated prior to the date of termination. This Agreement is subject to cancellation pursuant 
to A.R.S. § 38-511, the provisions of which are incorporated herein. 
 
In the event that the Federal agreement which provides funding for this award is paused and/or cancelled by a 
Federal agency, the Office of the Governor reserves the right to pause and/or cancel this award according to the 
same terms and timelines set by that Federal agency. 
 
Amendments to this Agreement shall not be valid unless made in writing and signed by both parties.  
 
If, prior to the completion of this Agreement, GVA  and Grantee mutually agree to terminate this Agreement,  
then GVA may, at its discretion, assign or award the remaining services and funding, in whole or in part, to 
another entity to complete substantially similar work and Grantee shall reasonably cooperate with such  
assignment or award, which may include, at GVA ’s discretion, execution by Grantee of an assignment or  
termination of this Agreement and forwarding of pertinent documentation to GVA and/or such other entity. 
 
IV. TERMINATION FOR CONVENIENCE 
Notwithstanding any provision of this Agreement to the contrary, GVA reserves the right to terminate this 
Agreement, in whole or in part at any time when in the best interest of the  State, without penalty or recourse. 
Upon receipt of written notice of termination pursuant to the preceding sentence, Grantee shall stop all work, as 
directed in the notice, notify all subcontractors or sub- grantees of the effective date of the termination and 
minimize all further costs related to this Agreement. In the event of  termination under this section, all 
documents, data and reports prepared by Grantee under this  Agreement shall become the property of and be 
delivered to GVA upon demand. Grantee shall be entitled to receive just and equitable compensation for work 
completed, and materials or services accepted before the effective date of the termination. 
 
Additionally, if GVA  determines the award contemplated in this Agreemen t was not properly awarded to  
Grantee, then GVA may terminate this Agreement for convenience and may, at its discretion, assign or award  
the remaining services and funding, in whole or in part, to another entity to complete substantially similar work. 
Grantee shall reasonably cooperate with such assignment or award, which may include, at GVA ’s discretion, 
execution by Grantee of an assignment or termination of this Agreement and forwarding of pertinent  
documentation to GVA and/or such other entity. 
 
V. DESCRIPTION OF SERVICES 
Grantee shall administer this funding to support the Renewables Coordinator in accordance with Attachment A 
– Budget and Scope of Work. 
 
VI. MANNER OF FINANCING 
The GVA shall: 
A. Provide to Grantee a total award amount of up to $270,111.00 for activities outlined in Section V. 
B. Transfer funding to Grantee upon receipt of valid invoices for reimbursement as the project is executed. 
Any unused funding pursuant to this agreement at the termination date shall be transferred back to the 
Office of the Governor and the remaining balance of the award de-obligated. 
C. The CFDA/Assistance Listing is 81.041 for all activity associated with this agreement. 
D. Questions regarding the appropriate use of the funds shall be resolved by mutual written agreement 
between Grantee and GVA. 
E. Notwithstanding any provisions of this Agreement to the contrary, if, prior to completion of this 
Agreement, GVA determines that Grantee does not have the capacity to fully perform the scope of 
services as outlined in this Agreement and the project proposals incorporated herein, then GVA may, at 
its discretion, assign or award the remaining services and funding, in whole or in part, to another entity 
to complete substantially similar work and Grantee shall reasonably cooperate with such assignment or 
award, which may include, at GVA’s discretion, execution by Grantee of an assignment or termination 
of this Agreement and forwarding of pertinent documentation to GVA and/or such other entity.

Grantee shall: 
A. Submit requests for reimbursement (invoices) for actual cash expenses incurred by the program. Grantee 
shall submit these requests for reimbursement at a minimum of quarterly but not more frequently than 
once per week.  
B. Assure that any  expenses reimbursed under this Agreement have not been or  will not be reimbursed 
under any other federal program.  
 
VII. REPORTING REQUIREMENTS 
Financial Reporting: 
The Grantee shall be paid on a cost-reimbursement basis. The Grantee shall not request reimbursement until 
the cost has resulted in an actual cash expenditure.  Grantee shall submit a final reimbursement request no 
more than forty-five (45) days after the Agreement end for expenses incurred prior to the date of Agreement 
termination. All expenses must be incurred and paid prior to the final reimbursement request. Requests for 
reimbursement received later than forty-five (45) days after the Agreement termination will not be paid. If 
awarded an agreement, your organization must have sufficient funds to meet obligations for up to sixty (60) 
days while awaiting reimbursements from the Office of the Governor. 
 
The Grantee shall use the forms provided by the GVA to submit financial expenditure reports. The forms 
will be sent to the Grantee upon receipt of the signed Agreement. 
 
Programmatic Reporting: 
Grantee shall submit programmatic reports to the Office of the Governor for activities performed under this 
Agreement. The report deadlines are as follows: 
A. Q2 2025 (June):   7/20/2025 
B. Q3 2025 (July to September):  10/20/2025 
C. Q4 2025 (October to December): 1/20/2026

J. Q3 2027 (July to September):
  10/20/2027 
 
Notwithstanding any other payment provision of this Agreement, failure of the Grantee to submit required 
reports when due, or failure to perform or deliver required work, supplies or services, will result in the 
withholding of payment under this Agreement unless such failure arises due to causes beyond the control 
and without the fault of negligence of the Grantee. 
 
All reports shall be submitted to the contact person designated in Section XXIII. 
 
VIII. DOCUMENTS INCORPORATED BY REFERENCE 
The State of Arizona’s Uniform Terms and Conditions Version 10.5 (“UTC”) is incorporated into this 
Agreement as if fully set forth herein. The Grantee warrants that it has read and understands the UTC and agrees 
to be bound to those applicable provisions in their entirety. In the event of any divergence between this 
Agreement and the UTC, this Agreement shall control. 
 
State of Arizona Uniform Terms and Conditions: 
https://spointra.az.gov/sites/default/files/Uniform%20Terms%20and%20Conditions_r10.5_03-24.pdf 
 
IX. FEDERAL UNIFORM REQUIREMENTS  
All grant awards made under this program shall comply with applicable laws including, but not limited to, the 
SEP statutory authority (42 U.S.C. § 6321 et seq.), 10 CFR 420, and 2 CFR 200 as amended by 2 CFR Part 910.

XI. PUBLICATIONS 
An acknowledgment of Federal support and a disclaimer must appear in the publication of any material, whether

XII. INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY 
The intellectual property provisions applicable to this Agreement are provided in the following link to the DOE 
website: https://www.energy.gov/gc/articles/ip-provisions-2023-5-11. 
 
XIII. AMERICAN-MADE EQUIPMENT AND PRODUCTS 
It is the sense of the United States Congress that, to the greatest extent practicable, all equipment and products 
purchased with funds made available under this award should be American-made.   
 
XIV. APPLICABLE LAW 
In accordance with A.R.S. § 41-2501, et seq. , and Arizona Administrative Code R2-7-101, et seq. , this 
Agreement shall be governed and interpreted by the laws of the State of Arizona and the Arizona Procurement 
Code. 
 
XV. NON-AVAILABILITY OF FUNDS 
In accordance with A.R.S. § 35-154, every payment obligation of the GVA under the Agreement is conditioned 
upon the availability of funds appropriated or allocated for payment of such obligation. If funds are not allocated 
and available for the continuance of this Agreement, this Agreement may be terminated by the GVA at the end 
of the period for which funds are available. No liability shall accrue to the GVA in the event this provision is 
exercised, and the GVA shall not be obligated or liable for any future payments or for any damages as a result 
of termination under this paragraph. 
 
XVI. AUDIT 
In accordance with A.R.S. § 35-214, Grantee shall retain and shall contractually require each contractor and 
subcontractor to retain all data, books and other records (“records”) relating to this Agreement for a period of 
five years after completion of the Agreement. All records shall be subject to inspection and audit by the GVA 
at reasonable times. Upon request, Grantee shall produce the original of any or all such records. 
 
XVII. CONFLICT OF INTEREST 
In accordance with A.R.S. § 38- 511, the GVA may within three years after execution cancel the Agreement,  
without penalty or further obligation, if any person significantly involved in initiating, negotiating, securing, 
drafting or creating the Agreement on behalf of the GVA, at any time while the Agreement is in effect, becomes

an employee or agent or any other party to the Agreement in any capacity or a consultant to any other party of 
the Agreement with respect to the subject matter of the Agreement. 
 
XVIII. FUND MANAGEMENT 
The Grantee must maintain funds received under this Agreement in separate ledger accounts and cannot mix 
these funds with other sources. The Grantee must manage funds according to applicable federal regulations for 
administrative requirements, cost principles and audits. 
  
The Grantee must maintain adequate business systems to comply with Federal requirements.  The business 
systems that must be maintained are: 
A. Financial Management 
B. Procurement 
C. Personnel 
D. Property 
E. Travel 
 
A system is adequate if it is: 1) written; 2) consistently followed - it applies in all similar circumstances; and 3) 
consistently applied – it applies to all sources of funds. The GVA reserves the right to review all business 
systems policies. 
 
XIX. UEI/SAM.GOV 
In accordance with FAR 4.1102, each recipient who is awarded $30,000 or more in Federal funding must 
provide the following prior to an Agreement being executed: (a) Unique Entity Identifier (UEI) number for the 
fiscal agent; and (b) proof of current registration in the SAM.gov (SAM) website. SAM registration must be 
maintained for the term of the Agreement. SAM registration information may be found at 
https://sam.gov/content/home
. 
 
XX. FFATA REPORTING REQUIREMENTS

Pub. L. No. 109-282, 120 Stat. 1186, as amended by Section 6202 (a) of Pub. L. No. 110- 252, the Grantee is 
required to provide information. The FFATA legislation requires information on federal awards (federal 
financial assistance and expenditures) to be made available to the public via a single, searchable website, which 
is www.USASpending.gov.

This Agreement may be changed to allow for revisions or additions to the scope of work contemplated herein,  
which may require an increase or decrease in anticipated expenses. GVA  and Grantee must mutually agree on 
any change order and GVA may, at its discretion, assign or award any remaining services and funding in whole

termination of this Agreement and forwarding of pertinent documentation to GVA and/or such other entity. 
 
XXII. FORCED LABOR BAN 
The Contractor certifies that it is in compliance with A.R.S.  § 35-394, which prohibits public entities from 
entering into contracts with companies that utilize any of the following: (1) The forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs 
in the People's Republic of China; (2) Any goods or services produced by the forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in 
the People's Republic of China; and (2) Any contractors, subcontractors or suppliers that use the forced labor or 
any goods or services produced by the forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the People's Republic of China. 
 
XXIII. NON-DISCRIMINATION

applicable Federal and State laws, rules and regulations, including the Americans with Disabilities Act.  These 
provisions will be included in contracts with Subcontractors when required by Federal or State law.

or willful acts or omissions of Grantee or any of its owners, officers, directors, agents, employees o r sub -
grantees. This indemnity includes any claim or amount arising out of, or recovered under, the Workers’ 
Compensation Law or arising out of the failure of such Grantee to conform to any federal, state, or local law, 
statute, ordinance, rule, regulation, or court decree. It is the specific intention of the parties that the Indemnitee 
shall, in all instances, except for Claims arising solely from the negligent or willful acts or omissions of the 
Indemnitee, be indemnified by Grantee from and against any a nd all claims. It is agreed that Grantee will be 
responsible for primary loss investigation, defense, and judgment costs where this indemnification is applicable. 
In consideration of the execution of this contract, the Grantee agrees to waive all rights of  subrogation against 
the State of Arizona, its officers, officials, agents, and employees for losses arising from the work performed by 
the Grantee for the State of Arizona. This indemnity shall not apply if the grantee or sub -grantee(s) is/are an

Scope of Work for the Economic Dev and Renewables Coordinator (Navajo County Coal Community 
Development Manager) 
Th
e Coal Community Development Manager will be a Non-Exempt (hourly), fixed-term 2-year position 
for Navajo County.  
The attached Navajo County Job Description details the Essential Job Functions, Knowledge and Skills, 
Desirable Qualifications and Position Location.  
The Job Description has been approved by Navajo County Human Resources and the appropriate salary 
range has been assigned to this role. Salary Range 133, ($67,614.82 – $84,441.64) 
Recruitment and on-boarding will be performed by Navajo County in alignment with HR Procedures and 
Policies.  
The Coal Community Development Manager will be directly supervised by Navajo County Economic 
Development Director, who also serves as the Executive Director of REAL AZ. The service area will be 
aligned with REAL AZ’s membership; unincorporated Navajo and Apache Counties and all municipal 
jurisdictional partners (Pinetop – Lakeside, Springerville, Eagar, St. Johns, Show Low, Snowflake, Taylor, 
Holbrook, and Winslow). 
The Budget for the Coal Community Development Manager is attached and will be accounted in a 
separate account designated exclusively for the role. All Navajo County financial policies and procedures 
will be applied to the expenses incurred.  
The 
role will meet the allowable uses of the State Energy Program (SEP) funding through addressing the 
following areas:  
➔ Increase and promote energy efficiency and renewable energy (renewable energy systems)
➔ Increase investments to expand the use of clean energy resources and infrastructure
➔ Support state and local governments in targeting underserved sectors and communities (e.g., small
businesses, rural communities) for energy projects.
➔ Program activities to quantify the co-benefits of energy efficiency and renewable energy, including
improvements in air quality, reductions in greenhouse gas emissions, improvements in public health and
economic development.
➔ Outreach to affected stakeholders

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/10/2025  
Title: Resignation of Pam Phillips as the Public Fiduciary Director and appt of Eileen Rogers 
Submitted By: Leah Thomas, Deputy Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Public Fiduciary
Presented By: Pam Phillips
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and acceptance of the resignation of Pamela Phillips as the Public Fiduciary Director and
appointment of Eileen Rogers as the new Public Fiduciary Director, effective 7/1/2025.
Background:
I will be retiring June 30, 2025 as the Public Fiduciary Director. My 19 plus years with the County
Attorney and the Public Fiduciary have been the best in my work career and I am grateful for the
opportunity I have had to serve those in need in our County. 
Recommendation: 
I respectfully request that the Board consider appointing Eileen Rogers as the next Public Fiduciary
Director. Eileen has worked in this department for 22 years, has been a case manager for 19 of those
years and has been the Deputy Director since January 2022. She is knowledgeable, experienced, licensed
by the AZ Supreme Court as a Fiduciary and is very qualified to serve as the Director.

Chairman Jason Whiting

Re: Letter of Resignation
Dear Chairman Whiting,
I will be retiring as the Public Fiduciary Director on June 30, 2025. As I have said many times, I have
been so blessed to have worked in this department serving the most vulnerable, incapacitated and
often exploited Navajo County residents. I thank the Board and the County Manager Bryan Layton
for the support and confidence in all of us working in this department.
I recommend that the Board approve Eileen Rogers as the next Public Fiduciary Director. Eileen
has been the Deputy Director for the past 3+ years, has been a case manager for 19 years, has
been with the Public Fiduciary for 22 years and a County employee for a total of 32 years. Eileen is
a licensed fiduciary who knows every facet of what we do. She will be a great leader and carry on
our department’s commitment and duty to serve.
Thank you again for trusting me to serve as the Public Fiduciary Director. It has been an honor and
challenge for me that I will never forget.
Respectfully,
Pam Phillips | Public Fiduciary Director

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/10/2025  
Title: Award of (WIOA) One-Stop Operator, Contract B25-04-003 to Head Forward Solutions
LLC.
Submitted For: Adam Garrard, WIA Executive Director 
Submitted By: Kolton Root, Sr. Procurement Officer
Department: Northeastern Az Innovative Workforce
Presented By: Adam Garrard
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act (WIOA) One-Stop Operator,
Contract B25-04-003 to Head Forward Solutions LLC.
Background:
The designation of a One Stop Operator is a requirement under the Workforce Innovation and
Opportunity Act (WIOA) Section 121(d). The role of the One Stop Operator is to oversee the One Stop
Delivery System. The One Stop Operator will be responsible for the operational activities concerning
customer flow and overall quality and effectiveness of the Comprehensive One Stop Job Center.  The
resulting contract would be in effect from July 1, 2025 through June 30, 2026 and may be renewed up to
4 one-year periods.
Sealed proposals for a One-Stop Operator were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo
County Governmental Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 pm (MST) May 21, 2025. No proposals were
accepted after 3:00 pm. The proposals were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 pm. Three proposals
were received and evaluated per the criteria set forth in the request for proposals. 
Recommendation: 
The evaluation committee is recommending the award of Contract No. B25-04-003 Workforce
Innovation and Opportunity Act (WIOA) One-Stop Operator to Head Foward Solutions LLC. as the
highest-ranking responsible, responsive offeror. The Local Workforce Development Board (LWDB) is in
concurrence.

NOTICE OF REQUEST FOR PROPOSALS 
RFP NO. B25 -04-003 WORKFORCE INNOVATION AND OPPORTUNITY ACT (WIOA) ONE -STOP 
OPERATOR 
 
Notice is hereby given that Navajo County, on behalf of the Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce 
Development Board, is conducting a competitive ONE-STEP process for the purpose of selecting a 
qualified Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act of 2014 (WIOA) One -Stop Operator for the 
Local Workforce Development Area. A copy of the WIOA and regulations may be accessed via the 
U.S. Department of Labor website ( https://www.dol.gov/agencies/eta/wioa). The term of this 
contract will be one (1) year with the option to renew up to (4) additional one-year terms. 
 
INTERESTED OFFERORS MAY OBTAIN A COPY OF THIS SOLICITATION BY CALLING (928) 524-4100 
OR MAY PICK UP A COPY OF THE PROPOSAL PACKAGE AT 100 W. PUBLIC WORKS DR., 
HOLBROOK, ARIZONA 86025, OR MAY DOWNLOAD THE PACKAGE AT: 
https://www.navajocountyaz.gov/571/Procurement---Solicitations. 
 
All Proposals for the specified service shall be received by the Navajo County Clerk of the Board of 
Supervisor’s Office at the location indicated, on or prior to the exact time and date indicated below. 
Late submittals shall not be considered.  The official prevailing clock is located in the Clerk of the 
Board of Supervisor’s Office.  Proposals received by the correct time and date shall be publicly 
recorded. Navajo County takes no responsibility for informing recipients of changes to the original 
solicitation document. Failure to acknowledge addendums with the solicitation response may be 
grounds for deeming the Proposal non-responsive. 
 
Proposals must be submitted in a sealed envelope.  The RFP number and the offeror’s name and 
address should be clearly indicated on the outside of the envelope. Failure to clearly indicate RFP 
number, name and address on the outside of the package or envelope may be cause for the 
Proposal to be deemed invalid. All Proposal’s must be completed in ink or typewritten.  Questions 
must be in writing and addressed to the contact listed below.  The last day to submit questions is 
May 14, 2025 at 12:00 P.M. local AZ Time.
